A free-form agentic optimizer with tools: evaluate, a Python sandbox, set_prompt, finish. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/lu2026spear) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.26275) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26275)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[Adaptive-GEPA](#/papers/chen2026adaptivegepa "Adaptive-GEPA: Make Your Harness Fit Heterogeneous Requests (2026)")**: Make Your Harness Fit Heterogeneous Requests (preprint 2026). Evolves a router and a library of specialist programs under one search budget. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/chen2026adaptivegepa) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.38762) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38762)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[C-Evolve](#/papers/li2025cevolve "C-Evolve: Consensus-based Evolution for Prompt Groups (2025)")**: Consensus-based Evolution for Prompt Groups (preprint 2025). Evolves a group of prompts whose majority vote is best, with island-based diversity. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/li2025cevolve) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.23331) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23331)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Maestro](#/papers/wang2025maestro "Maestro: Joint Graph & Config Optimization for Reliable AI Agents (2025)")**: Joint Graph & Config Optimization for Reliable AI Agents (preprint 2025 (RELAI technical report)). Jointly searches an agent's graph (modules, flow) and each node's configuration. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wang2025maestro) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.04642) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04642)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[Feedback Descent](#/papers/lee2025feedbackdescent "Feedback Descent: Open-Ended Text Optimization via Pairwise Comparison (2025)")**: Open-Ended Text Optimization via Pairwise Comparison (preprint 2025). Optimizes text artifacts (prompts, code, molecules) with pairwise comparisons that carry textual critiques. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/lee2025feedbackdescent) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.07919) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07919)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[CORE (Contrastive Reflection)](#/papers/nasvytis2026core "CORE: Contrastive Reflection Enables Rapid Improvements in Reasoning (2026)")**: CORE: Contrastive Reflection Enables Rapid Improvements in Reasoning (preprint 2026). Compares successful and unsuccessful reasoning traces and distills the differences into short natural-language insights (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/nasvytis2026core) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.28742) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28742) · [Code](https://github.com/LinasNas/core-reasoning)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[BudgetAPO](#/papers/liu2026budgetapo "How Should a Prompt Optimizer Spend a Tight Budget? BudgetAPO with Noise-Adaptive Evaluation (2026)")**: How Should a Prompt Optimizer Spend a Tight Budget? BudgetAPO with Noise-Adaptive Evaluation (preprint 2026). A single-stage prompt optimizer for a tight budget of subject-model calls: a 16-call probe measures the task's per-row score spread, a closed-form rule sizes a fixed evaluation slice to a target standard error, every candidate is scored on that same slice so each accept/reject is a paired comparison, and an optimizer LLM rewrites the prompt's reasoning strategy and output format from up to three failing rows (abstract; §3.1–3.3). The authors call it the first APO method to size its evaluation to each task's measured noise before search (§1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2026budgetapo) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05671) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05671)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>

<a id="does-prompt-optimization-help-evidence-and-analyses"></a>

#### Does prompt optimization help? Evidence and analyses

- **[Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip](#/papers/zhang2026coinflip "Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip: Diagnosing When It Helps in Compound AI Systems (2026)")**: Diagnosing When It Helps in Compound AI Systems (CTB@ICML 2026 (workshop)). Runs six optimizers on four single-agent tasks, 3 repeats, two executor models (§4, §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhang2026coinflip) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.14585) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14585)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[Skill Issue](#/papers/kozyrev2026skillissue "Skill Issue: Lessons from Optimizing Repository SKILLs for Coding Agents (2026)")**: Lessons from Optimizing Repository SKILLs for Coding Agents (preprint 2026). Optimizes repository SKILL files for coding agents on tasks mined from real pull requests. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/kozyrev2026skillissue) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12742) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12742)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[MAGE](#/papers/singh2026mage "MAGE: Understanding Stability-Performance Trade-offs in Multi-component Prompt Optimization (2026)")**: Understanding Stability-Performance Trade-offs in Multi-component Prompt Optimization (preprint 2026). A controlled platform for how memory, Pareto selection and adaptive evaluation interact in reflective optimization. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/singh2026mage) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.11944) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11944)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[What Should the Reflector See?](#/papers/zhou2026reflector "What Should the Reflector See? An Empirical Study of Evidence in Reflective Prompt Optimization (2026)")**: What Should the Reflector See? An Empirical Study of Evidence in Reflective Prompt Optimization (preprint 2026). Compares nine reflection strategies (evidence composition, example visibility, candidate selection, knowledge policy; abstract) in a GEPA-inspired search. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhou2026reflector) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.32452) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32452)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[Benchmarking Prompt Optimization of Large Language Models With Chess](#/papers/lesort2026chess "Benchmarking Prompt Optimization of Large Language Models With Chess (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of 1,118 Lichess puzzles for prompt optimization of frozen LLMs (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/lesort2026chess) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.00416) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00416)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-misc">promptopt-misc</a></span>
- **[Which Self-Improvements Should We Trust? Reliable Self-Improvement When Agents Reuse Their Benchmarks](#/papers/sun2026reuse "Which Self-Improvements Should We Trust? Reliable Self-Improvement When Agents Reuse Their Benchmarks (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A certified evaluation and promotion rule for self-improvement loops that reuse one fixed evaluation set: with probability at least 1−α every promoted change is a genuine improvement on the task distribution (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/sun2026reuse) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.33180) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33180)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[Do Agent Optimizers Compound?](#/papers/wang2026compound "Do Agent Optimizers Compound? A Continual-Learning Evaluation on Terminal-Bench 2.0 (2026)")**: Do Agent Optimizers Compound? A Continual-Learning Evaluation on Terminal-Bench 2.0 (technical report 2026 (RELAI)). A two-phase continual-learning test on hard Terminal-Bench 2.0 tasks: optimize, then optimize again after new tasks arrive (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wang2026compound) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.14004) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14004) · [Code](https://github.com/relai-ai/Continual-Learning-Terminal-Bench)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[What Survives the Next Model?](#/papers/salsabil2026survives "What Survives the Next Model? Benchmarking LLM-Based Techniques Against Single-Prompts (2026)")**: What Survives the Next Model? Benchmarking LLM-Based Techniques Against Single-Prompts (preprint 2026). Tests whether one automatically generated prompt on a newer model beats 35 LLM-based software-engineering techniques from ICSE 2026 (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/salsabil2026survives) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00468) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00468) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ICSE2027-What_Survives_The_Next_Model)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[SkillsBench](#/papers/li2026skillsbench "SkillsBench: Benchmarking How Well Agent Skills Work Across Diverse Tasks (2026)")**: Benchmarking How Well Agent Skills Work Across Diverse Tasks (preprint 2026). A paired benchmark of agent skills: tasks across domains with curated skills and deterministic verifiers, each run with and without skills on many model–harness configurations (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/li2026skillsbench) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.12670) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12670)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[The Winner's Curse in LLM Self-Improvement Loops: Selection Noise, Lock-in, and Acceptance Rules](#/papers/hu2026winnerscurse "The Winner's Curse in LLM Self-Improvement Loops: Selection Noise, Lock-in, and Acceptance Rules (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Models the keep-if-better step of self-improvement loops as selection under measurement noise, with candidates of a generation sharing the incumbent's error, and studies what reusing the selection set does (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/hu2026winnerscurse) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.09239) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09239)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[Is This LLM Library Learning? Evaluation Must Account For Compute and Behaviour](#/papers/berlotattwell2025libraryfails "Is This LLM Library Learning? Evaluation Must Account For Compute and Behaviour (2026)")** (EACL 2026). Re-runs three in-context library-learning systems, LEGO-Prover (Isabelle lemmas, miniF2F), TroVE and AgentOptimizer (Python functions and tools, MATH and table QA), and measures whether what they learn is reused directly (all three, §3.1–3.2) or by editing (LEGO-Prover only, §3.2) (abstract; §1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/berlotattwell2025libraryfails) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.03048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03048) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.eacl-long.163) · [Code](https://github.com/ikb-a/llm_lib_learning_fails)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[PACE: Anytime-Valid Acceptance Tests for Self-Evolving Agents](#/papers/shawn2026pace "PACE: Anytime-Valid Acceptance Tests for Self-Evolving Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Treats the keep-it-if-the-dev-score-went-up step of self-evolving agents as uncontrolled adaptive multiple testing on a reused dev set, and replaces it with a commit gate: candidate and incumbent are scored on the same items, and a testing-by-betting e-process on the discordant pairs commits once its wealth reaches 1/α (abstract; §3; §4). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/shawn2026pace) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08106) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08106)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-misc">promptopt-misc</a></span>
- **[When Does Continual Learning Require Learning](#/papers/harrington2026continual "When Does Continual Learning Require Learning (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Compares ways to keep improving an LLM as its tasks change, under one staged protocol on Qwen3-8B: prompt optimization (GEPA, ACE), supervised updates (SFT, SDFT), reinforcement learning (GRPO, SDPO) and context compression (Cartridges, In-place TTT) (abstract; §3.2, Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/harrington2026continual) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.07847) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07847) · [Code](https://github.com/anneharrington/studying-cl)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[LangProBe: a Language Program Benchmark](#/papers/tan2025langprobe "LangProBe: a Language Program Benchmark (2025)")** (Findings of EMNLP 2025). A benchmark of language programs: DSPy programs from a single call and chain of thought to RAG, multi-hop retrieval, ReAct agents and generator–critic–ranker or –fuser pipelines (§3.2), crossed with four prompt optimizers (BootstrapFewShot, BootstrapFewShotRandomSearch, MIPROv2, and the authors' RuleInfer, §3.3) and six OpenAI and Llama models (§4), on 16 tasks in six categories, among them AppWorld, HumanEval, MATH, GSM8K, classification and question answering (§3.1; Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/tan2025langprobe) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.20315) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20315) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-emnlp.1172) · [Code](https://github.com/Shangyint/langProBe)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-misc">promptopt-misc</a></span>
- **[Rethinking the Evaluation of Harness Evolution for Agents](#/papers/wang2026harnesseval "Rethinking the Evaluation of Harness Evolution for Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v4)). Re-evaluates automatic harness evolution, where an LLM agent rewrites an agent's prompts, tools, memory and control code from task feedback (as Meta-Harness, AHE and AEVO do), against parallel sampling and sequential refinement at a matched budget, with and without unit-test feedback, on Terminal-Bench 2.1 with Claude Opus 4.6, GPT-5.4 and GPT-5.4 mini (abstract; §3; §4.1). It adds harness scaling, its own per-task variant that rewrites the harness for each task (§3.5). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wang2026harnesseval) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.12227) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12227) · [Code](https://github.com/rethinking-harness-evolution/code)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[What Does a Harness Repair? A Preregistered Study of Visibility, Baseline Adequacy and Evaluation Defects](#/papers/xu2026harnessrepair "What Does a Harness Repair? A Preregistered Study of Visibility, Baseline Adequacy and Evaluation Defects (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A preregistered study of where the gains of harness search come from, a search that keeps a change to the prompts, reasoning switches, token budgets or parsers around a frozen model if it raises a score: three small models (a longevity-tuned Qwen3-1.7B, SmolLM3-3B, Intern-S1-mini) on LongevityBench, MMLU-Pro and GSM8K pools with replication and test partitions, and a GEPA arm that searches prompt text only (abstract; §1; §3.1; Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/xu2026harnessrepair) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05533) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05533)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Are Online Skill and Memory Modules Always Worth Their Tokens? A Budget-Constrained Study of Web Agents](#/papers/hajimiri2026worthtokens "Are Online Skill and Memory Modules Always Worth Their Tokens? A Budget-Constrained Study of Web Agents (2026)")** (EMNLP 2026). Re-evaluates three online memory, workflow and skill modules for web agents (AWM, ASI, ReasoningBank) against a vanilla actor that spends a comparable token budget on more interaction steps instead (Vanilla-IB), counting the tokens of every module (abstract; §1; §3.2–3.3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/hajimiri2026worthtokens) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15017) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15017)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>
- **[The Regression Tax](#/papers/tank2026regressiontax "The Regression Tax: Decomposing Why Skills Help -- and Hurt -- LLM Agents (2026)")**: Decomposing Why Skills Help -- and Hurt -- LLM Agents (preprint 2026). Splits the pass-rate change from adding a skill library into gains (tasks newly solved) and regressions (tasks a no-skill agent solved and now fails), with exact McNemar tests (abstract; §3.5–3.6; §4). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/tank2026regressiontax) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.22520) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22520) · [Code](https://github.com/sentient-agi/meta-skill-creator)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a></span>

<a id="verifiers-reward-models-and-judges-beyond-sql"></a>

### Verifiers, reward models and judges beyond SQL

- **[GSM8K and trained verifiers](#/papers/cobbe2021verifiers "Training Verifiers to Solve Math Word Problems (2021)")**: Training Verifiers to Solve Math Word Problems (preprint 2021). Introduces GSM8K and trains a verifier on final-answer correctness labels to rank sampled solutions (§4.2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/cobbe2021verifiers) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.14168) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.14168) · [Code](https://github.com/openai/grade-school-math)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Let's Verify Step by Step](#/papers/lightman2023verify "Let's Verify Step by Step (2023)")** (preprint 2023). Process supervision (each step labeled) beats outcome supervision for reward models, on MATH (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/lightman2023verify) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.20050) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.20050) · [Code](https://github.com/openai/prm800k)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Generative Verifiers (GenRM)](#/papers/zhang2024genrm "Generative Verifiers: Reward Modeling as Next-Token Prediction (2025)")**: Generative Verifiers: Reward Modeling as Next-Token Prediction (ICLR 2025). Reward modeling as next-token prediction: the verifier answers "Yes"/"No" (§3.1), in its CoT variant after writing a rationale (§3.3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhang2024genrm) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.15240) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15240)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[JudgeBench](#/papers/tan2024judgebench "JudgeBench: A Benchmark for Evaluating LLM-based Judges (2025)")**: A Benchmark for Evaluating LLM-based Judges (ICLR 2025). A benchmark of LLM judges on response pairs whose correctness is objectively checkable. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/tan2024judgebench) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.12784) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12784) · [Code](https://github.com/ScalerLab/JudgeBench)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[LLMs as a Jury](#/papers/liu2026jury "LLMs as a Jury: Cross-Model Consensus Can Outperform Process Reward Models for LLM Reasoning (2026)")**: Cross-Model Consensus Can Outperform Process Reward Models for LLM Reasoning (preprint 2026). Agreement across independently trained models, each solving once, picks the answer from a Best-of-N pool; the authors report it beats self-consistency and self-scoring on seven benchmarks (Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2026jury) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.10139) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10139)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Weaver](#/papers/saadfalcon2025weaver "Shrinking the Generation-Verification Gap with Weak Verifiers (2025)")**: Shrinking the Generation-Verification Gap with Weak Verifiers (NeurIPS 2025). Builds a strong verifier from many weak ones (LM judges, reward models) to rank sampled answers (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/saadfalcon2025weaver) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18203) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18203)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Don't Judge Code by Its Cover](#/papers/moon2025codejudge "Don't Judge Code by Its Cover: Exploring Biases in LLM Judges for Code Evaluation (2025)")**: Exploring Biases in LLM Judges for Code Evaluation (EACL 2026 Findings, 2025). Tests whether LLM judges grade code consistently under six injected, semantics-preserving biases: authority, self-declared correctness, reverse-authority and misleading-task comments, variable renaming, and unused dummy functions (§1, §3); the abstract also names formatting, which isn't tested. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/moon2025codejudge) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.16222) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16222)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[EvalPlus](#/papers/liu2023evalplus "Is Your Code Generated by ChatGPT Really Correct? Rigorous Evaluation of Large Language Models for Code Generation (2023)")**: Is Your Code Generated by ChatGPT Really Correct? Rigorous Evaluation of Large Language Models for Code Generation (NeurIPS 2023). Augments code benchmarks with generated tests (HumanEval+: 80× the tests of HumanEval) (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2023evalplus) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.01210) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.01210) · [Code](https://github.com/evalplus/evalplus)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[ImpossibleBench](#/papers/zhong2025impossiblebench "ImpossibleBench: Measuring LLMs' Propensity of Exploiting Test Cases (2025)")**: Measuring LLMs' Propensity of Exploiting Test Cases (preprint 2025). Variants of LiveCodeBench and SWE-bench tasks whose tests conflict with the spec; the authors say any pass implies a specification-violating shortcut (abstract), and validate the LLM-made mutations on SWE-bench only (§2.3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhong2025impossiblebench) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.20270) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20270) · [Code](https://github.com/safety-research/impossiblebench)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[Code Monitor Red Teaming](#/papers/liao2026codemonitor "Code Monitor Red Teaming for Public-Test-Passing Code (2026)")**: Code Monitor Red Teaming for Public-Test-Passing Code (preprint 2026). Can a weaker LLM verifier find hidden bugs in code that already passes public tests? (abstract) <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liao2026codemonitor) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.20852) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20852)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[unearned passes in agentic benchmarks](#/papers/luo2026unearned "Maintaining Benchmarks Against Increasingly Capable Agents: Detection and Remediation of Unearned Passes (2026)")**: Maintaining Benchmarks Against Increasingly Capable Agents: Detection and Remediation of Unearned Passes (preprint 2026). Audits passing agent trajectories to separate reward hacking from verifier weakness (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/luo2026unearned) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.34262) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34262)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[REFUTE ("Can Language Models Falsify?")](#/papers/sinha2025refute "Can Language Models Falsify? Evaluating Algorithmic Reasoning with Counterexample Creation (2025)")**: Can Language Models Falsify? Evaluating Algorithmic Reasoning with Counterexample Creation (preprint 2025). A benchmark of LLMs creating counterexamples for subtly incorrect algorithmic solutions, checked by running code (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/sinha2025refute) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.19414) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19414) · [Code](https://github.com/falsifiers/REFUTE)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairs-code">pairs-code</a></span>
- **[Disproving Program Equivalence with LLMs](#/papers/allamanis2025disproving "Disproving Program Equivalence with LLMs (2025)")** (preprint 2025). A white-box search for counterexamples to the equivalence of two or more executable programs, by an LLM with execution feedback (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/allamanis2025disproving) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.18473) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18473)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Program Semantic Inequivalence Game with Large Language Models](#/papers/micelibarone2025sinq "Program Semantic Inequivalence Game with Large Language Models (2025)")** (NeSy 2026 (PMLR vol. 284, per the v3 PDF's first-page header), 2025). A generator makes semantically different variants of real programs and an evaluator must find inputs on which they differ; the two train each other (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/micelibarone2025sinq) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03818) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03818) · [Code](https://github.com/Avmb/semantic_neq_game)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[Round-Trip Correctness (RTC)](#/papers/allamanis2024roundtrip "Unsupervised Evaluation of Code LLMs with Round-Trip Correctness (2024)")**: Unsupervised Evaluation of Code LLMs with Round-Trip Correctness (ICML 2024). Evaluates code LLMs without human labels: describe code in natural language, synthesize code back, and check whether the result is semantically equivalent to the original (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/allamanis2024roundtrip) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08699) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08699) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/icml2024-roundtrip-correctness)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[Mind the Gap](#/papers/song2024mindgap "Mind the Gap: Examining the Self-Improvement Capabilities of Large Language Models (2025)")**: Examining the Self-Improvement Capabilities of Large Language Models (ICLR 2025). A controlled study of self-improvement where a model verifies its own outputs, filters or reweights them, and distills (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/song2024mindgap) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.02674) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02674)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[On the Self-Verification Limitations of Large Language Models on Reasoning and Planning Tasks](#/papers/stechly2024selfverification "On the Self-Verification Limitations of Large Language Models on Reasoning and Planning Tasks (2024)")** (preprint 2024). GPT-4 critiquing its own answers vs a sound external verifier, on Game of 24, graph coloring and STRIPS planning (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/stechly2024selfverification) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08115) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08115)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Engineering Reliable Commit Gates for Agentic AI: Cost-Aware Verification Portfolios under Common-Mode Data Failures](#/papers/zheng2026vpcontrol "Engineering Reliable Commit Gates for Agentic AI: Cost-Aware Verification Portfolios under Common-Mode Data Failures (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A gate between an agent's proposed state-changing action and its execution chooses a verification plan (re-check on the same source, another verifier model, an independent read, a transactional guard, deferral to a person) from metadata a deployment can observe, calibrated to a risk target (abstract; §III). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zheng2026vpcontrol) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.10969) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10969) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33511441.v1) · [Code](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33511441.v1)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[Correlated Errors in Large Language Models](#/papers/kim2025correlated "Correlated Errors in Large Language Models (2025)")** (ICML 2025). Measures how often different LLMs make the same mistake: the rate at which two models give the same wrong answer when both are wrong, over hundreds of models on MMLU questions with labelled answers from the HuggingFace Open LLM Leaderboard and HELM, plus the correlation of residuals against hand labels on LLM resume ratings (abstract; §1; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/kim2025correlated) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.07962) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07962) · [Code](https://github.com/nikhgarg/llm_correlated_errors_public)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[Self-Improvement in Language Models: The Sharpening Mechanism](#/papers/huang2024sharpening "Self-Improvement in Language Models: The Sharpening Mechanism (2025)")** (ICLR 2025). Formalizes self-improvement as "sharpening": the model acts as its own verifier (a self-reward such as its own likelihood) to shift probability onto high-quality responses, amortizing inference-time search into post-training (abstract; §1.1, §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/huang2024sharpening) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.01951) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01951)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[UOJ-Bench ("Beyond Problem Solving")](#/papers/xu2026uojbench "Beyond Problem Solving: UOJ-Bench for Evaluating Code Generation, Hacking, and Repair in Competitive Programming (2026)")**: Beyond Problem Solving: UOJ-Bench for Evaluating Code Generation, Hacking, and Repair in Competitive Programming (ICML 2026 (PMLR 306, per the PDF's first-page footer)). A benchmark of three competitive-programming tasks built from real submissions to the Universal Online Judge (UOJ): code generation, code hacking (write an input that breaks a buggy solution) and code repair, all graded by UOJ's own judge through its API (abstract; §1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/xu2026uojbench) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.12864) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12864) · [Code](https://github.com/hehezhou/UOJ-Bench)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Semantic Equivalence Self-Play (SEQ/SINQ](#/papers/poon2026selfplay "Improving LLM Code Reasoning via Semantic Equivalence Self-Play with Formal Verification (2026)")**: Improving LLM Code Reasoning via Semantic Equivalence Self-Play with Formal Verification (Haskell), Findings of ACL 2026). A generator (Alice) writes a variant of a reference Haskell program, either equivalent with a Liquid Haskell proof (SEQ) or inequivalent with a diverging input that is executed (SINQ); an evaluator (Bob) judges whether the pair is equivalent, and its sampled success rate sets each instance's difficulty (abstract; §3.1, §3.3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/poon2026selfplay) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17010) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17010) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.findings-acl.1615) · [Code](https://github.com/Baki-0501/llm-self-play-liquidhaskell)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[TrickCatcher](#/papers/liu2024trickcatcher "LLM-Powered Test Case Generation for Detecting Bugs in Plausible Programs (2025)")**: LLM-Powered Test Case Generation for Detecting Bugs in Plausible Programs (ACL 2025). Generates tests that expose bugs in "plausible programs", those that pass an existing test suite yet are wrong (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2024trickcatcher) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.10304) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10304) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.acl-long.20) · [Code](https://github.com/RinCloud/TrickCatcher)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[When to Trust the Cheap Check](#/papers/kiyani2026weakstrong "When to Trust the Cheap Check: Weak and Strong Verification for Reasoning (2026)")**: Weak and Strong Verification for Reasoning ((weak and strong verification, SSV), preprint 2026). A policy decides for each output whether to accept or reject it on a cheap "weak" verifier's score (self-consistency, proxy rewards) or to defer to a costly "strong" verifier (a user, a check against ground truth); its errors are incorrect acceptance (type I) and incorrect rejection (type II) relative to the strong verdict, plus how often the strong verifier is called (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/kiyani2026weakstrong) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.17633) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17633) · [Code](https://github.com/nooranisima/weak-and-strong-verification)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Commit-first LLM judging inherits the judge's own errors](#/papers/gozel2026commitfirst "Commit-first LLM judging inherits the judge's own errors (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Audits the default LLM-judge configurations of eight evaluation frameworks for commit-first judging (in the author's definition, the judge commits its own answer before it sees the candidate and accepts only a match (abstract); in [More Convincing, Not More Correct](#/papers/zhou2026convincing "More Convincing, Not More Correct: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (2026)"), commit-first keeps the candidate in view and blind-solve withholds it (App. A)): the author reports that none of the 24 in-scope configurations implements it and nine use what it calls the "recompute" form, which that work reports ineffective (abstract; §2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/gozel2026commitfirst) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00088) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00088) · [Code](https://github.com/idilgozel/evaluator-integrity)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[More Convincing, Not More Correct](#/papers/zhou2026convincing "More Convincing, Not More Correct: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (2026)")**: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (preprint 2026). Self-play against a reference-free LLM judge: one model answers GSM8K questions, judges its own answers without a reference, and is trained by DPO on the judge's accept/reject pairs, while a hidden anchor, an exact-match check on the final answer that the judge never sees, audits true accuracy (abstract; §3; §5). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhou2026convincing) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.05904) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05904)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Scaling Laws for Reward Model Overoptimization](#/papers/gao2022overoptimization "Scaling Laws for Reward Model Overoptimization (2023)")** (ICML 2023). A fixed 6B "gold" reward model stands in for human labellers and labels comparisons that train proxy reward models of 3M to 3B parameters; a GPT-3-series policy in the InstructGPT setting is optimized against the proxy by best-of-n or by PPO, and the gold score is tracked as optimization grows (abstract; §2, §2.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/gao2022overoptimization) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.10760) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.10760)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>

### Reinforcement learning from verifiable rewards

- **[DeepSeekMath](#/papers/shao2024deepseekmath "DeepSeekMath: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models (2024)")**: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models (preprint 2024). Math pretraining on web data, then RL. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/shao2024deepseekmath) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03300) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03300) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Math)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[DeepSeek-R1](#/papers/deepseek2025reasoning "DeepSeek-R1: Incentivizing Reasoning Capability in LLMs via Reinforcement Learning (2025)")**: Incentivizing Reasoning Capability in LLMs via Reinforcement Learning (Nature 645 (2025)). Reasoning learned by RL with rule-based rewards (accuracy and format), starting without SFT (R1-Zero). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/deepseek2025reasoning) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09422-z) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>

(2025)")** (NeurIPS 2025 (oral)). - **[CATCH](#/papers/wang2026catch "CATCH: A Controllable Analysis Testbed for Reward Hacking in Coding RL (2026)")**: A Controllable Analysis Testbed for Reward Hacking in Coding RL (preprint 2026). When does reward rise while correctness falls under an imperfect verifier? (abstract) <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/moya2026verifiererrors) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35677) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35677) · [Code](https://github.com/cmoyacal/verifier-errors)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>

A monitor for reward hacking in self-evolving loops that attaches through two black-box hooks (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/yang2026hackprobe) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04665) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04665)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-misc">promptopt-misc</a></span>
