Prolonged RL training with KL control, reference-policy resets and a diverse task suite (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2025prorl) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.24864) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24864) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/085713-0608)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards Implicitly Incentivizes Correct Reasoning in Base LLMs](#/papers/wen2025rlvr "Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards Implicitly Incentivizes Correct Reasoning in Base LLMs (2025)")** (preprint 2025). Revisits pass@k for math and code, and introduces CoT-Pass@K, which also requires correct reasoning steps, judged by an LLM (DeepSeek-R1-0528-Qwen3-8B, §1, §3.1) (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wen2025rlvr) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14245) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14245)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[When the Reward Suite Is Leaky](#/papers/zhang2026leaky "When the Reward Suite Is Leaky: A Preregistered Causal Contrast of Natural Verifier False Positives in RLVR (2026)")**: A Preregistered Causal Contrast of Natural Verifier False Positives in RLVR (preprint 2026). A preregistered two-arm contrast: GRPO on MBPP rewarded by the original tests (leaky) or by the MBPP+ extra tests alone (hardened; not the official base-and-extra metric, §3.1), with the same tasks, seeds and compute (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhang2026leaky) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.11022) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11022) · [Code](https://github.com/toffee-desuwa/rlvr-leaky-suite)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[An Imperfect Verifier is Good Enough](#/papers/plesner2026imperfect "An Imperfect Verifier is Good Enough: Learning with Noisy Rewards (2026)")**: Learning with Noisy Rewards (preprint 2026). RLVR on code (MBPP; Qwen3 8B and GLM4 9B, with Llama 3.1 8B and Qwen3 4B ablations; most configurations single-seed, §4) with injected reward noise of controlled rate and structure, and model-based verifiers as rewards (abstract; §3.2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/plesner2026imperfect) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.07666) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07666)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[LLMs Gaming Verifiers](#/papers/helff2026gaming "LLMs Gaming Verifiers: RLVR can Lead to Reward Hacking (2026)")**: RLVR can Lead to Reward Hacking (LLM Reasoning Workshop @ ICLR 2026 (PDF header)). RLVR-trained models on inductive rule tasks enumerate instance labels instead of inducing a rule, which passes a verifier that checks only extensional correctness (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/helff2026gaming) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15149) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15149) · [Code](https://github.com/ml-research/llms-gaming-verifiers)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Teacher-Free Self-Training Amplifies but Does Not Compound: A Pass@$K$ Crossover on a Free-Verifier Domain](#/papers/strozzi2026selftraining "Teacher-Free Self-Training Amplifies but Does Not Compound: A Pass@$K$ Crossover on a Free-Verifier Domain (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A generator and a learned critic (LoRA adapters on one 4-bit Qwen3-4B) with the DSL interpreter as a free exact verifier, on a FlashFill-style "trapdoor" DSL (abstract; §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/strozzi2026selftraining) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.07856) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07856) · [Code](https://github.com/izzortsi/trapdoor-loom)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Language Models Can Teach Themselves to Program Better](#/papers/haluptzok2022teach "Language Models Can Teach Themselves to Program Better (2022)")** (ICLR 2023 (the PDF's running header, p. 1), 2022). An LM writes programming puzzles and solutions, a Python interpreter keeps the correct ones, and the LM is fine-tuned on them (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/haluptzok2022teach) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.14502) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.14502)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[The Verifier is the Curriculum](#/papers/zhou2026curriculum "The Verifier is the Curriculum: Precision Sets the Return on Search in Code Self-Distillation (2026)")**: Precision Sets the Return on Search in Code Self-Distillation (preprint 2026). Rejection-sampling self-distillation of a code generator, gated by whether the generated game project launches cleanly, with no judge (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhou2026curriculum) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09709) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09709)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[STaR (Self-Taught Reasoner)](#/papers/zelikman2022star "STaR: Bootstrapping Reasoning With Reasoning (2022)")**: STaR: Bootstrapping Reasoning With Reasoning (NeurIPS 2022). A few-shot-prompted LLM writes rationales for many questions; only those whose final answer matches the dataset's answer are kept, the model is fine-tuned on them, and the loop repeats (abstract; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zelikman2022star) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.14465) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.14465) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/068431-1126) · [Code](https://github.com/ezelikman/STaR)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[Self-Play Only Evolves When Self-Synthetic Pipeline Ensures Learnable Information Gain](#/papers/liu2026infogain "Self-Play Only Evolves When Self-Synthetic Pipeline Ensures Learnable Information Gain (2026)")** ((position paper), ICML 2026 (position track)). Argues that self-evolving loops in which one LLM plays proposer, solver and verifier plateau because the loop "synthesises more data without increasing learnable information" (abstract), and proposes three designs: asymmetric co-evolution, capacity growth and proactive information seeking (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2026infogain) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02218) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02218)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[Delay, Plateau, or Collapse](#/papers/egashira2026systematic "Delay, Plateau, or Collapse: Evaluating the Impact of Systematic Verification Error on RLVR (2026)")**: Evaluating the Impact of Systematic Verification Error on RLVR ((systematic verification error in RLVR), COLM 2026 (the PDF's running header, p. 1)). Separates random verifier noise, which flips rewards independently of the output, from systematic errors, which depend on the query and the output, so a model can learn to trigger or avoid them; it names three outcomes against clean training: delay, plateau and collapse (§3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/egashira2026systematic) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.02909) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02909) · [Code](https://github.com/eth-sri/llm-verifier-noise)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[Noisy Data is Destructive to Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards](#/papers/zhu2026noisyrlvr "Noisy Data is Destructive to Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v2)). Re-examines claims that RLVR learns almost as well from wrong annotations: the authors re-verify the "incorrect" math training set of [Spurious Rewards](#/papers/shao2025spurious "Spurious Rewards: Rethinking Training Signals in RLVR (2025)") with GPT-5 Pro, `math-verify`, a GPT-5 judge and manual checks, and remove the annotations that are in fact correct (abstract; §1; §3.3, Fig. 2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhu2026noisyrlvr) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.16140) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16140)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-data">nl2sql-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[RLVεR ("Rate or Fate?")](#/papers/rad2026rlver "Rate or Fate? RLV$^\varepsilon$R: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Noisy Rewards (2026)")**: Rate or Fate? RLV$^\varepsilon$R: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Noisy Rewards (preprint 2026). Models GRPO under a verifier with false positives and false negatives as a multi-armed bandit over recurring "reasoning modes", which gives a replicator-style flow on the probability simplex (abstract; §2.1; §4; §5). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/rad2026rlver) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.04411) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04411) · [Code](https://github.com/cognichip/Noisy-RL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[Prompt Dominance and Asymmetric Verifier Costs: Empirical Ablations of GRPO at 1B Scale on GSM8K](#/papers/hou2026grpo "Prompt Dominance and Asymmetric Verifier Costs: Empirical Ablations of GRPO at 1B Scale on GSM8K (2026)")** ((GRPO at 1B on GSM8K), preprint 2026). A from-scratch GRPO implementation trains OLMo-2-0425-1B on GSM8K with a symbolic-equality grader as the reward, with prompt and learning-rate ablations, one-change estimator variants (Dr. GRPO among them) and off-policy corrections (clipped, unclipped, GSPO); two seeds per configuration except the learning-rate sweep and the verifier study's exact arm (abstract; §2–5; Fig. 2; §6.3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/hou2026grpo) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.04928) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04928) · [Code](https://github.com/Helios-YQH/llm-from-scratch)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>

<a id="llm-theorem-provers-beyond-sql"></a>

### LLM theorem provers beyond SQL

- **[COPRA](#/papers/thakur2023copra "An In-Context Learning Agent for Formal Theorem-Proving (2024)")**: An In-Context Learning Agent for Formal Theorem-Proving (COLM 2024). GPT-4 proposes tactics from inside a stateful backtracking search, in Lean and Coq (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/thakur2023copra) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.04353) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.04353) · [Code](https://github.com/trishullab/copra)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[LeanDojo](#/papers/yang2023leandojo "LeanDojo: Theorem Proving with Retrieval-Augmented Language Models (2023)")**: Theorem Proving with Retrieval-Augmented Language Models (NeurIPS 2023 (Datasets and Benchmarks)). An open toolkit that extracts proof data from Lean and lets programs interact with it, plus ReProver, a retrieval-augmented prover (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/yang2023leandojo) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.15626) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.15626) · [Code: LeanDojo](https://github.com/lean-dojo/LeanDojo) · [Code: ReProver](https://github.com/lean-dojo/ReProver)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Baldur](#/papers/first2023baldur "Baldur: Whole-Proof Generation and Repair with Large Language Models (2023)")**: Whole-Proof Generation and Repair with Large Language Models (ESEC/FSE 2023). Fine-tuned LLMs generate whole Isabelle/HOL proofs at once, instead of one step at a time inside a search (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/first2023baldur) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.04910) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.04910) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3611643.3616243)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-general">prove-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-itp">prove-itp</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Rango](#/papers/thompson2024rango "Rango: Adaptive Retrieval-Augmented Proving for Automated Software Verification (2025)")**: Adaptive Retrieval-Augmented Proving for Automated Software Verification (ICSE 2025). Coq proof synthesis for software verification with a fine-tuned LLM that retrieves relevant premises and similar proofs from the current project at every step (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/thompson2024rango) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.14063) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14063) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE55347.2025.00161) · [Code](https://github.com/rkthomps/coq-modeling)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-general">prove-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-itp">prove-itp</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[DeepSeek-Prover-V2](#/papers/ren2025deepseekproverv2 "DeepSeek-Prover-V2: Advancing Formal Mathematical Reasoning via Reinforcement Learning for Subgoal Decomposition (2025)")**: Advancing Formal Mathematical Reasoning via Reinforcement Learning for Subgoal Decomposition (preprint 2025). Lean 4 provers (671B, abstract; 7B, §2.3) trained with RL after a cold start (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/ren2025deepseekproverv2) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.21801) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21801) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Prover-V2)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Goedel-Prover-V2](#/papers/lin2025goedelproverv2 "Goedel-Prover-V2: Scaling Formal Theorem Proving with Scaffolded Data Synthesis and Self-Correction (2025)")**: Scaling Formal Theorem Proving with Scaffolded Data Synthesis and Self-Correction (preprint 2025). Open Lean 4 prover models trained by expert iteration and RL (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/lin2025goedelproverv2) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.03613) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03613) · [Code](https://github.com/Goedel-LM/Goedel-Prover-V2)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Seed-Prover](#/papers/chen2025seedprover "Seed-Prover: Deep and Broad Reasoning for Automated Theorem Proving (2025)")**: Deep and Broad Reasoning for Automated Theorem Proving (preprint 2025). A lemma-style Lean whole-proof model that refines its proof from Lean feedback, proved lemmas and self-summaries (abstract), trained with RL (§2.2.3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/chen2025seedprover) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.23726) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23726) · [Code](https://github.com/ByteDance-Seed/Seed-Prover)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Quarry ("Planning to Hammer")](#/papers/zhang2026quarry "Planning to Hammer: Difficulty-Aware Decomposition for Automating Rocq Proofs (2026)")**: Planning to Hammer: Difficulty-Aware Decomposition for Automating Rocq Proofs (preprint 2026 (submitted to OOPSLA 2026)). The LLM proposes decompositions of a Rocq goal into sublemmas, and CoqHammer discharges the pieces (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhang2026quarry) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.17981) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17981) · [Code](https://github.com/ningZhang-cs/QUARRY)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-general">prove-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-itp">prove-itp</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[Harnessing Code Agents for Automatic Software Verification](#/papers/kan2026aria "Harnessing Code Agents for Automatic Software Verification (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Hands each Coq lemma to a general code agent (e.g. Claude Code) inside a verification harness, with no fixed proof strategy (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/kan2026aria) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.06341) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06341) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/CoqProver-Code-57BB)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-general">prove-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-itp">prove-itp</a></span>
- **[STP](#/papers/dong2025stp "STP: Self-play LLM Theorem Provers with Iterative Conjecturing and Proving (2025)")**: Self-play LLM Theorem Provers with Iterative Conjecturing and Proving (ICML 2025). One model is both conjecturer and prover: conjectures barely provable by the current prover train the conjecturer, and the prover uses expert iteration (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/dong2025stp) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.00212) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00212) · [Code](https://github.com/kfdong/STP)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[Lemur](#/papers/wu2023lemur "Lemur: Integrating Large Language Models in Automated Program Verification (2024)")**: Integrating Large Language Models in Automated Program Verification (ICLR 2024). Combines LLMs and automated reasoners for program verification: LLM oracle calls propose and repair properties, which the verifier checks (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wu2023lemur) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.04870) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.04870) · [Code](https://github.com/wu-haoze/Lemur-program-verification)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-general">prove-general</a></span>
- **[LEGO-Prover](#/papers/wang2023legoprover "LEGO-Prover: Neural Theorem Proving with Growing Libraries (2024)")**: Neural Theorem Proving with Growing Libraries (ICLR 2024). Proves Isabelle theorems with ChatGPT block by block: a decomposer proposes sub-goal lemmas, a formalizer writes the proof using lemmas retrieved from a growing skill library, and an evolver generalizes library lemmas and proves the requested sub-goals (abstract; §3; ChatGPT, §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wang2023legoprover) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.00656) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.00656) · [Code](https://github.com/wiio12/LEGO-Prover)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-general">promptopt-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>

<a id="evaluating-llm-methods-statistics-labels-and-compact-models"></a>

### Evaluating LLM methods: statistics, labels and compact models

- **[Deep Reinforcement Learning at the Edge of the Statistical Precipice](#/papers/agarwal2021rliable "Deep Reinforcement Learning at the Edge of the Statistical Precipice (2021)")** ((rliable), NeurIPS 2021). Argues that comparisons of deep RL methods from a handful of runs per task must report uncertainty: stratified bootstrap confidence intervals, performance profiles, and the interquartile mean (IQM) as aggregate (Tab. 1; §4). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/agarwal2021rliable) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.13264) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.13264) · [Code](https://github.com/google-research/rliable)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a></span>
- **[How Much Can Language Models Gain from Test-Time Computation?](#/papers/yang2026selfpot "How Much Can Language Models Gain from Test-Time Computation? (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark and evaluation framework for test-time scaling across competition mathematics, AtCoder programming, AppWorld workflows and Terminal-Bench tasks, on a test set sealed before any test-time call (abstract; §2.4, Tab. 1). Each task is graded by a rule-based answer grader, hidden tests or the benchmark's own final-state evaluator (§2.5). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/yang2026selfpot) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.01110) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01110)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-general">scaling-general</a></span>
- **[The Optimizer's Curse](#/papers/smith2006optimizerscurse "The Optimizer's Curse: Skepticism and Postdecision Surprise in Decision Analysis (2006)")**: Skepticism and Postdecision Surprise in Decision Analysis (Management Science 2006). Shows that choosing the alternative with the highest estimated value makes that estimate biased high even when every estimate is unbiased, so a decision maker should expect to be disappointed on average (abstract; §2.3, Prop. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/smith2006optimizerscurse) · [PDF](https://jimsmith.host.dartmouth.edu/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/The_Optimizers_Curse.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.1050.0451)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a></span>
- **[Who Judges the Judge: An Empirical Study on Online Judge Tests](#/papers/liu2023onlinejudge "Who Judges the Judge: An Empirical Study on Online Judge Tests (2023)")** (ISSTA 2023). Audits AtCoder's hidden test suites on 939 problems and 541,552 accepted Python, Java and C++ solutions (from CodeContests): their line and branch coverage and mutation scores, and whether accepted solutions are still wrong (abstract; §3.1–3.2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2023onlinejudge) · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3597926.3598060) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3597926.3598060) · [Code](https://zenodo.org/records/7977256)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a></span>
- **[Which Decisions Low-Bit Quantization Breaks, and How to Predict Them](#/papers/wu2026lowbit "Which Decisions Low-Bit Quantization Breaks, and How to Predict Them (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v5)). Reads decisions of 16 open models at the first token, as the logit margin between two options (call a tool or reply in text, which tool, which answer, refuse or comply), before and after post-training quantization to 4, 3 and 2 bits under round-to-nearest, activation-aware scaling, GPTQ and GGUF, on BFCL, MetaTool, XSTest, MMLU, BoolQ, BBQ and authored items (abstract; §2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wu2026lowbit) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.06564) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06564)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[Quantization Effects on Tool-Failure Recovery Vary Across Prompts and Evaluation Designs](#/papers/hu2026toolrecovery "Quantization Effects on Tool-Failure Recovery Vary Across Prompts and Evaluation Designs (2026)")** (NeurIPS 2026 SLM-Agents workshop). Compares 8-bit and 4-bit GGUF variants of Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct as tool-calling agents recovering from one injected transient tool failure, on twenty deterministic order-management tasks with verified answers and five prompts (abstract; §4). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/hu2026toolrecovery) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.07781) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07781)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[DISCERN ("Pay Only for Disagreement")](#/papers/balachandran2026discern "Pay Only for Disagreement: Certified No-Regression Verdicts for Model Updates with Matching Label-Complexity Bounds (2026)")**: Pay Only for Disagreement: Certified No-Regression Verdicts for Model Updates with Matching Label-Complexity Bounds (preprint 2026). Certifies whether a model update (retrain, fine-tune, quantization, vendor swap) is a regression: a sequential audit of the paired risk difference between old and new model, with anytime-valid confidence sequences, valid under any predictable label-routing rule (abstract; §4; §5.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/balachandran2026discern) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.17560) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17560)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[SWE-ABS: Adversarial Benchmark Strengthening Exposes Inflated Success Rates on Test-based Benchmark](#/papers/yu2026sweabs "SWE-ABS: Adversarial Benchmark Strengthening Exposes Inflated Success Rates on Test-based Benchmark (2026)")** (ICML 2026 (per the authors' repo)). Strengthens SWE-Bench test suites in two stages: tests aimed at patch-relevant lines found by program slicing, then LLM-made mutants of the gold patch that pass the existing tests, with new tests written to reject them (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/yu2026sweabs) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.00520) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00520) · [Code](https://github.com/OpenAgentEval/SWE-ABS)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[Phantom Gains](#/papers/xu2026phantomgains "Phantom Gains: Auditing Self-Improvement Against a Measured Null (2026)")**: Auditing Self-Improvement Against a Measured Null (preprint 2026 (under review, venue not named)). Audits per-problem claims about self-improvement (which problems a model learned, lost or newly reached) by passing a frozen, untrained model through the same pipeline as every trained arm, so that any transition the frozen model shows is a measurement artifact (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/xu2026phantomgains) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.20290) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20290) · [Code](https://github.com/chengxuphd/phantom-gains)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-general">rlvr-general</a></span>
- **[Coding Agents as Test-Suite Auditors](#/papers/xie2026testauditors "Coding Agents as Test-Suite Auditors: Finding What Official Suites Miss While Approaching What They Catch (2026)")**: Finding What Official Suites Miss While Approaching What They Catch (preprint 2026). Off-the-shelf coding agents (Codex CLI, Claude Code, Antigravity, OpenCode, mini-SWE-agent) build adversarial test suites from a problem's statement and one reference solution, without seeing the official tests or the submissions audited (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/xie2026testauditors) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.01715) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01715) · [Code](https://github.com/xieTwim/test-suite-auditors)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking](#/papers/ammanamanchi2026leandefects "Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking: Dataset Defects and Evaluation Failures in Lean Theorem Proving (2026)")**: Dataset Defects and Evaluation Failures in Lean Theorem Proving (ICML 2026). Audits five Lean theorem-proving benchmarks (miniF2F, ProofNet, FormalMath, CombiBench, ProverBench) and their forks, with a fault taxonomy: fidelity failures in formalization, evaluation-time loopholes, and maintenance decay (abstract; §1; Tab. 1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/ammanamanchi2026leandefects) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29493) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29493) · [Code](https://github.com/Shashi456/atp-checkers)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[SWE-Proof: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World Issues with Machine-Checked Proofs?](#/papers/ma2026sweproof "SWE-Proof: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World Issues with Machine-Checked Proofs? (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v2)). A benchmark that gives each of the 500 SWE-bench Verified issues a formal specification, a verifying reference implementation and its proof under three backends (Nagini for Python, Velvet in Lean, and pure Lean with agent-written proofs), extended to the Python instances of SWE-bench Pro (abstract; §1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/ma2026sweproof) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21190) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21190)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/itp-general">itp-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[ModelEquivBench: Certifying Multi-Relational Evaluation of LLM-Generated Optimization Models](#/papers/zhu2026modelequivbench "ModelEquivBench: Certifying Multi-Relational Evaluation of LLM-Generated Optimization Models (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Grades an LLM-generated optimization model against a reference not with one equivalent/not-equivalent verdict but with a profile of seven relations, E0–E6: it builds and ingests, a verified variable map, feasible sets (same space or under an affine lift), objective order, optimal value, and optimizer sets (abstract; §1; Tab. 1). The authors say it is an evaluator, "not a new benchmark dataset": the problems come from Bench4Opt (§1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhu2026modelequivbench) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.29431) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29431)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-general">cex-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-llm">cex-llm</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-general">prove-general</a></span>
- **[VeRA: Renewing Reasoning Benchmarks with Executable Specifications](#/papers/cheng2026vera "VeRA: Renewing Reasoning Benchmarks with Executable Specifications (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v2, a rewrite of v1)). Turns each item of a reasoning benchmark into an executable task family, a question template, an input generator and a deterministic answer program, so that new labelled instances come from running code; VeRA-E draws fresh instances of the same family, VeRA-H modifies the family toward harder tasks, and VeRA-H Pro keeps the judge-ranked best of up to five validated proposals per seed (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/cheng2026vera) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.13217) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13217) · [Code](https://github.com/Marco-Cheng/VeRA)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[Pervasive Label Errors in Test Sets Destabilize Machine Learning Benchmarks](#/papers/northcutt2021labelerrors "Pervasive Label Errors in Test Sets Destabilize Machine Learning Benchmarks (2021)")** (NeurIPS 2021 (Datasets and Benchmarks)). Finds label errors in the test sets of 10 common image, text and audio datasets, among them ImageNet, CIFAR-10, IMDB and AudioSet: confident learning flags likely errors from a model's out-of-sample predicted probabilities, and five Mechanical Turk workers judge each flagged example, or a random sample of them for QuickDraw and Amazon Reviews (abstract; §3; §4; Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/northcutt2021labelerrors) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.14749) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.14749)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a></span>
- **[Finding Blind Spots in AppWorld and WorkArena Task Verifiers](#/papers/abrich2026blindspots "Finding Blind Spots in AppWorld and WorkArena Task Verifiers (2026)")** (NeurIPS 2026 workshop "Who Verifies the Agents?"). Audits the shipped execution-based checkers of two agent benchmarks for false accepts: AppWorld's state-based unit tests over the application database and WorkArena's live validators on a hosted ServiceNow instance (abstract; §3.2). No model is called; AppWorld replays reference solutions and WorkArena runs the shipped drivers' `cheat` (§2; App. H; Reproducibility statement). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/abrich2026blindspots) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.09142) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09142)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a></span>
- **[Auditing Reward Hackability in Code RL Training Environments](#/papers/rajan2026hackability "Auditing Reward Hackability in Code RL Training Environments (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Measures how often a code task's test suite accepts a wrong solution: Claude Sonnet 4 writes incorrect patches meant to pass the existing tests, in up to three rounds that read the failure logs, and each is run with the project's own test runner in the task's Docker container; a sample of SWE-bench Verified tasks from two repositories (astropy, django) and of R2E-Gym tasks (abstract; §3.1; §4; §7). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/rajan2026hackability) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16062) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16062)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>
- **[Towards Reliable LLM Evaluation: Correcting the Winner's Curse in Adaptive Benchmarking](#/papers/xu2026siren "Towards Reliable LLM Evaluation: Correcting the Winner's Curse in Adaptive Benchmarking (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A reporting protocol for scores after prompt or program search. Its target is the fresh-data performance of the whole tune-then-deploy procedure at each tuning budget, not the winning artifact's score (§2). It freezes the shortlist the search returned and, on a separate evaluation pool, repeatedly splits the items into a scoring part that sets smooth selection weights (softmax by default, §3.3, §4) and a held-out part that scores the result, with an item-level Gaussian multiplier bootstrap for intervals and simultaneous bands over budgets (abstract; §3.1–3.2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/xu2026siren) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05973) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05973) · [Code](https://github.com/jefferyz001/siren)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[Position: Don't Use the CLT in LLM Evals With Fewer Than a Few Hundred Datapoints](#/papers/bowyer2025clt "Position: Don't Use the CLT in LLM Evals With Fewer Than a Few Hundred Datapoints (2025)")** (ICML 2025 (spotlight position paper)). Argues that error bars from the central limit theorem fail for LLM evals with few questions, with clustered questions, when comparing two models, and for metrics that are not averages such as F1, and recommends Wilson score or Bayesian Beta–Bernoulli intervals for one model on independent questions and Bayesian models for clustered and paired data (abstract; §3.1–3.5). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/bowyer2025clt) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.01747) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01747) · [Code: bayes_evals](https://github.com/sambowyer/bayes_evals) · [Code: no_clt_paper](https://github.com/sambowyer/no_clt_paper)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[How Sensitive Are LLM Leaderboard Claims to Hidden Model Selection?](#/papers/yang2026leaderboard "How Sensitive Are LLM Leaderboard Claims to Hidden Model Selection? (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v2)). Asks how many privately scored sibling variants a published leaderboard margin over a fixed comparator can survive: under a Gaussian margin model it derives the least-favorable null of the selected margin and inverts it into a per-claim sensitivity curve, the largest hidden count k̄ as a function of a lower bound on the siblings' correlation (abstract; §1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/yang2026leaderboard) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28177) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28177)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[Noise Floor Audit for Agent Benchmarks](#/papers/chen2026noisefloor "Noise Floor Audit for Agent Benchmarks (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Measures how much the score of a tool-calling endpoint moves between identical reruns at temperature 0 and under semantics-preserving surface changes to the user message (collapsed whitespace, or an added prefix, wrapper or suffix), on a frozen set of 150 matched instances from BFCL's AST-graded `multiple` and `parallel` categories (abstract; §3; Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/chen2026noisefloor) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.22331) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22331)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[Generalization in Adaptive Data Analysis and Holdout Reuse](#/papers/dwork2015holdout "Generalization in Adaptive Data Analysis and Holdout Reuse (2015)")** (NeurIPS 2015). Gives algorithms that let an analyst validate many adaptively chosen hypotheses against one holdout set without overfitting to it: Thresholdout answers with the training-set value unless it differs from the holdout value by more than a noisy threshold, so a hypothesis that does not overfit reveals little about the holdout set; SparseValidate answers arbitrary Boolean queries and is analysed by description length (abstract; §1.1; §4.1–4.2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/dwork2015holdout) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1506.02629) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1506.02629)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a></span>
- **[Measuring all the noises of LLM Evals](#/papers/wang2025evalnoise "Measuring all the noises of LLM Evals (2025)")** (preprint 2025 (v2)). Splits the noise of an LLM eval into prediction noise (different answers to the same question), data noise (which questions were sampled) and their total, by the law of total variance, and proposes the "all-pairs paired method": paired analysis on every pair of models, measuring all three components (abstract; §1; §2.1; §3.2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wang2025evalnoise) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21326) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21326) · [Code](https://github.com/all-the-noises/eval-arena)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[A Sober Look at Progress in Language Model Reasoning: Pitfalls and Paths to Reproducibility](#/papers/hochlehnert2025sober "A Sober Look at Progress in Language Model Reasoning: Pitfalls and Paths to Reproducibility (2025)")** (COLM 2025). Measures how far math-reasoning scores of open 1.5B and 7B reasoning models move with evaluation choices alone: random seed, temperature and top-p, output-token limit, prompt template, hardware and evaluation framework, on AIME'24, AMC'23 and MATH500 (§3.1–3.4). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/hochlehnert2025sober) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.07086) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07086) · [Code](https://github.com/bethgelab/sober-reasoning)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/general-misc">general-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-misc">llm-misc</a></span>
- **[What Fits (Into Few Tokens) Doesn't Overfit: Compression and Generalization in ML Research Agents](#/papers/bertran2026compression "What Fits (Into Few Tokens) Doesn't Overfit: Compression and Generalization in ML Research Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Asks why reusing a validation set adaptively causes little overfitting, and tests the hypothesis that successful strategies are compressible on LLM research agents (Claude Opus in Claude Code) that edit and train models on 8 tasks, from tabular classification to language and diffusion modeling, scored on a separate holdout (abstract; §4, Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/bertran2026compression) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11045) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11045)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-general">hacking-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-general">harness-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a></span>

Self-play fine-tuning (SPIN) for text-to-SQL, with the execution results of predicted SQL fed into the parameter update (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/yan2025exspin) · [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/1099-4300/27/3/235/pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.3390/e27030235)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-data">nl2sql-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rlvr-sql">rlvr-sql</a></span>
- **[Measuring Crystallization in Text-to-SQL](#/papers/wang2026crystallization "From Test-Time Scaling to Reusable Memory: Measuring Crystallization in Text-to-SQL (2026)")**: From Test-Time Scaling to Reusable Memory: Measuring Crystallization in Text-to-SQL (preprint 2026). Measures the later value of stored, verified repair episodes: replay, cross-question retention, and held-out transfer on the same database, with the single-shot solver held fixed (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wang2026crystallization) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.07213) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07213) · [Code](https://github.com/ai-jiaqian/text-to-sql-memory-crystallization)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/hacking-sql">hacking-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-sql">harness-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-select">nl2sql-select</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-sql">scaling-sql</a></span>
- **[Spider 2.0](#/papers/lei2024spider2 "Spider 2.0: Evaluating Language Models on Real-World Enterprise Text-to-SQL Workflows (2025)")**: Evaluating Language Models on Real-World Enterprise Text-to-SQL Workflows (ICLR 2025). Enterprise text-to-SQL workflows over real databases in local and cloud systems such as BigQuery and Snowflake (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/lei2024spider2) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.07763) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07763) · [Code](https://github.com/xlang-ai/Spider2)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-sql">harness-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-eval">nl2sql-eval</a></span>
- **[BIRD-INTERACT](#/papers/huo2025birdinteract "BIRD-INTERACT: Re-imagining Text-to-SQL Evaluation for Large Language Models via Lens of Dynamic Interactions (2026)")**: Re-imagining Text-to-SQL Evaluation for Large Language Models via Lens of Dynamic Interactions (ICLR 2026). Multi-turn text-to-SQL: each database comes with a knowledge base, metadata files and a function-driven user simulator, so a model can ask for clarification, retrieve knowledge and recover from errors (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/huo2025birdinteract) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05318) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05318) · [Code](https://github.com/bird-bench/BIRD-Interact)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-sql">harness-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-misc">nl2sql-misc</a></span>
- **[Certified Against Which Oracle?](#/papers/liu2026whichoracle "Certified Against Which Oracle? Execution Labels Set the Reported Risk of Conformal Abstention for Text-to-SQL (2026)")**: Certified Against Which Oracle? Execution Labels Set the Reported Risk of Conformal Abstention for Text-to-SQL (preprint 2026). A preregistered intervention on a conformal abstention certificate for text-to-SQL (self-consistency over execution-equivalence classes, split-half conformal risk control): the clusters and the calibration labels come either from the benchmark's shipped database or from the distilled test suite of [Semantic Evaluation for Text-to-SQL…](#/papers/zhong2020testsuite "Semantic Evaluation for Text-to-SQL with Distilled Test Suites (2020)") (§1; §3.1–3.3), on Spider-Realistic with SQL-specialist checkpoints (§3.4). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2026whichoracle) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.25938) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25938) · [Code](https://github.com/yahiko-l/text-to-sql-oracle-certificates)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-data">nl2sql-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-eval">nl2sql-eval</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-select">nl2sql-select</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/scaling-sql">scaling-sql</a></span>
- **[Understanding the Effects of Noise in Text-to-SQL: An Examination of the BIRD-Bench Benchmark](#/papers/wretblad2024noise "Understanding the Effects of Noise in Text-to-SQL: An Examination of the BIRD-Bench Benchmark (2024)")** (ACL 2024 (short papers)). Two authors annotate every question and gold query of BIRD's financial-domain dev set (106 pairs) and 20 sampled pairs from each of four other domains for six kinds of noise, among them spelling or syntax errors, vague questions and incorrect gold SQL (§3.1–3.2; Tab. 1–2). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wretblad2024noise) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.12243) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12243) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-short.34) · [Code](https://github.com/niklaswretblad/the-effects-of-noise-in-text-to-SQL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-data">nl2sql-data</a></span>
- **[SALUS](#/papers/zhou2026salus "SALUS: Automated Auditing of NL-to-SQL Benchmarks through Weak Supervision of Multi-Agent Output (2026)")**: Automated Auditing of NL-to-SQL Benchmarks through Weak Supervision of Multi-Agent Output (SIGMOD 2027 (PACMMOD 4(6)), 2026). Flags wrong gold SQL in a text-to-SQL benchmark without human labels: four LLM agents (in the primary configuration GPT-5, GPT-4o, Claude Opus 4.6 and Gemini 2.5 Pro; §5.1) each write one query per task; 44 labeling functions over execution results, ASTs and question cues vote on the gold query; a Snorkel label model turns the votes into weak labels, and per-agent reliability models over 12 features of the gold SQL weight each agent's verdict (abstract; §3; §5.1, Tab. 3). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhou2026salus) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05540) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05540) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3856369) · [Code](https://github.com/ucisharadlab/SALUS)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-sql">judge-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-data">nl2sql-data</a></span>
- **[MIRA (Memory-Item Reuse and Adaptation)](#/papers/liu2026mira "MIRA: Evidence-Verified Repair Memory for Text-to-SQL Correction (2026)")**: MIRA: Evidence-Verified Repair Memory for Text-to-SQL Correction (preprint 2026). A corrector for SQL from a text-to-SQL agent treated as a black box (§2.1) that reuses past confirmed corrections on the same database without weight updates: it splits each past correction into separate repair memory items, retrieves items for a new query, activates only those the question, the SQL and database observations support, and adapts them to the current SQL (abstract; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/liu2026mira) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.06950) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06950)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-promptopt">harness-promptopt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/harness-sql">harness-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-sql">judge-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-misc">nl2sql-misc</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>

### Equivalence judges (LLM or learned) and benchmarks

- **[LLM-SQL-Solver](#/papers/zhao2023llmsqlsolver "LLM-SQL-Solver: Can LLMs Determine SQL Equivalence? (2025)")**: Can LLMs Determine SQL Equivalence? (IEEE BigData 2025). Asks whether LLMs can judge semantic and relaxed SQL equivalence. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zhao2023llmsqlsolver) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.10321) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.10321) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/BigData66926.2025.11401595) · [Code](https://github.com/ZhaoFuheng/LLM-SQL-Solver)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-sql">judge-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-eval">nl2sql-eval</a></span>
- **[Can the Rookies Cut the Tough Cookie? Exploring the Use of LLMs for SQL Equivalence Checking](#/papers/singh2024sqlequiquest "Can the Rookies Cut the Tough Cookie? Exploring the Use of LLMs for SQL Equivalence Checking (2024)")** (preprint, 2024). A harder, realistic equivalence benchmark; most LLMs are biased towards "equivalent", GPT-4 excepted (§5). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/singh2024sqlequiquest) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.05561) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05561) · [Code](https://github.com/rajatb115/LLMs-for-SQL-Equivalence-Checking)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-sql">judge-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairs-check">pairs-check</a></span>
- **[Taming SQL Complexity](#/papers/zeng2025taming "Taming SQL Complexity: LLM-Based Equivalence Evaluation for Text-to-SQL (2025)")**: LLM-Based Equivalence Evaluation for Text-to-SQL (preprint Jun 2025). An LLM judges semantic and "weak" semantic equivalence for text-to-SQL. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/zeng2025taming) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.09359) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09359)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-sql">judge-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2sql-eval">nl2sql-eval</a></span>
- **[EquiBench](#/papers/wei2025equibench "EquiBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models' Reasoning about Program Semantics via Equivalence Checking (2025)")**: Benchmarking Large Language Models' Reasoning about Program Semantics via Equivalence Checking (EMNLP 2025). Program-pair benchmark across 4 languages testing LLM reasoning about equivalence. <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/wei2025equibench) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.12466) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12466) · [Code](https://github.com/Anjiang-Wei/EquiBench)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-general">judge-general</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-method">llm-method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairs-code">pairs-code</a></span>
- **[GEqO](#/papers/haynes2024geqo "GEqO: ML-Accelerated Semantic Equivalence Detection (2023)")**: ML-Accelerated Semantic Equivalence Detection (PACMMOD 1(4) (SIGMOD 2024), 2023). Detects semantically equivalent SPJ subexpressions, aiming at cloud-scale workloads, for computation reuse; evaluated only on synthetic TPC-H/TPC-DS workloads (§7). <span class="links">[🔎 Summary](#/papers/haynes2024geqo) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.01280) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01280) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3626710)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/judge-sql">judge-sql</a></span>
